| 2026-09-04 | fu92-427-431-connector-credential-outages | **[[FU92-427]] Trade Desk + [[FU92-431]] Microsoft Ads — two credential outages root-caused, nothing deployed.** Ran as an `inquest` 5-seat council; every load-bearing claim re-verified by the orchestrator. **FU92-427 cause PROVEN: the static `TTD-Auth` token has a 365-day lifespan and expired 2026-08-05T20:54:29Z** — three independent instruments agree (Cosmos `_ts` = `1754427269`; the token's own embedded protobuf issuance field, 2025-08-05T20:45:51Z; git `clients@f31f0113`, 21:14:37Z), and +365d falls inside the last-success/first-401 bracket (16:22:56Z → 22:24:18Z). Expired, **not** revoked. Answers [[FU92-416]], open since 2026-05-21. **⭐ Three of the ticket's own premises refuted.** (a) The *"hard deadline ~2026-09-04, permanent loss"* traces to one hedged clause in one template comment, present on only one of the two templates, whose **measured** successor (*"30-45 day limit"*, `b32153a9`) merge `c5cd9818` silently reverted — and our own warehouse shows **one load session on 2024-09-24 writing 268 continuous days, zero gaps, a 272-day backdate**. The loss is **rolling, not a cliff**: missing 09-04 costs nothing, ~09-05 onward costs one day per day. (b) *"Unrecoverable"* — the **identical 401 outage ran 2025-08-01→08-05 and recovered 12/12 partition dates** with `schedule_id` unchanged, rescued within ~4h of the token being replaced. ⚠ Caveat recorded: that outage was **5 days**, this one **29** — mechanism proven, 29-day execution survival is **not**. (c) *"86 failures each"* was **1808/1824**. **Recovery is armed, proven by direct read**: TTL retirement is gated on a *completed* run (`route_work.py:1259`) so nothing retired — **60 partitions are `active`/`in_queue`** and self-retry, with a built-in positive control (**941 partitions on the same template ARE retired**, so the probe discriminates). Consumer layer: `CURRENT_*` is **full history** (14,665,156 rows, 935 days from 2023-12-27), max data date **2026-08-04**, gap **29 days**; renders as **absent rows, not false zeros** — negative control `Direct-Trade_Desk` 16 days before / **0** after vs every other platform 16/28. Exposure **~$4,558 / ~1.13M impressions** (DERIVED, 29 × $157.17/day); ⚠ **the July baseline implies ~$24,524 and is 5.4× too high** — spend fell legitimately on 08-01 as 13 of 16 campaigns ended at month-end. **FU92-431 cause PROVEN and the obvious fix is wrong**: the Function App presents a **March-expired client secret** while `Eclipse Secret 2026-05-22` (valid to 2028) sits unused; **90 error traces across 90 days, exactly one per day**. A new AD secret was created 2026-05-22T19:17:37Z and a refresh token minted **59 seconds later**, but `MICROSOFT_ADS_CLIENT_SECRET` was never updated — the token rotted unused until it died of 90-day inactivity. **Rotating the token alone repeats May's failure.** Two of my own hypotheses (AD secret expired; timer disabled) were **refuted by the instrument**. **⛔ Safety finding: `work_template_delete` calls `warehouse_reset` → `DROP TABLE`** — deleting the Eclipse template destroys **14,665,156 rows / 935 days**, and it is the *intuitive* way to repoint a `schedule_id`. Use `work_template_update` only. 🔴 **The live prod TTD token is committed in plaintext** to `clients/FUSION_92/eclipse/connections/trade_desk_my_reports.json` — repo value == prod value, verified by salted hash. **Credential absent from Key Vault (both vaults enumerated), every repo, and the wiki vault** — all three measured with proven-live instruments (the vault scan's positive control returned Microsoft Ads, Lectric SP-API, Google Ads); Dashlane was the only place left and Paul reports **4 Trade Desk records**, types/dates not yet triaged. **Method lessons, all paid for in-session:** ⭐ **`az monitor app-insights query` honours `--offset`, NOT `ago()` inside the KQL** — my "90-day" queries silently ran against ~1 hour, reading **150** traces where the true figure is **230,903**; caught only because a 7-day count (168) exceeded a 90-day count (150), which is impossible. ⭐ **The App Insights `requests` table does not reliably capture timer executions** — a function on a 30-minute schedule showed **1** run in 30 days; concluding "the refresh never ran" from its absence would have been the reported root cause. **`traces` is the table that sees timers.** ⭐ `summarize` with `max(timestamp)` fails through that CLI with `BadArgumentError` — drop datetime aggregates. ⭐ **The council corrected my own brief twice** — a "second earlier error" I flagged as a precursor was a 35-day-window artifact of a separate January defect, and a template "asymmetry" was a truncated-sweep read artifact; both came from seats explicitly licensed to reject the frame. **Also found, each now ticketed or flagged:** [[FU92-431]] Microsoft Ads (18 dates missing), FU92-432 Viant Reach & Frequency **dead since 2026-04-25, four months**, Snowflake network-policy IP block ~553 failures/day; | 2026-09-03 | gp254-lectric-status-audit | **[[GP-254]] read-only status audit of the Lectric agency feed — dataset, API connector, and TEST⨝PROD parity. Nothing deployed.** Triggered by a client question (how to hand Lectric sales to Justin). **⭐ The headline is a corrected premise, twice.** (a) Paul and I both concluded from the template's bounded `min_date=2026-07-01` that Lectric was "only an API connection test" with no history. **The data says otherwise: 5,310 order lines · 5,008 orders · 29 ASINs · 5,713 units · $2,822,972 gross · 2024-05-31 → 2026-09-03** — 27 months of real trading data. Two loads coexist (the one-off backfill measured at 4,464 rows on 2026-06-19, plus the scheduled connector appending ~840 since July); **the connector extends a dataset it did not create.** A bounded `min_date` is a fact about the *feed*, never about the *data*. (b) [[GP-254]]'s own 2026-06-17 note (*"`Order Line` has no entity key and does not contain Lectric at all"*) is now stale — the Option-C native fix landed in TEST, `ENTITY_CODE` exists and Lectric is in the fact. **Parity verdict: the ONLY column difference between TEST and PROD is `ENTITY_CODE`** (118 vs 117 cols; PROD-only: none; shared 117), PROD has no `AMAZON_LECTRIC` schema at all, and Navira ties across environments (TEST 3,551,296 lines/$131,714,461 vs PROD 3,548,562/$131,632,262 over top-12 companies = 0.06%, the truncated remainder). Per Paul this is **expected — "we don't promote past test"** — so it is a clean pass, not a gap; but it means `WHERE ENTITY_CODE='LECTRIC'` against PROD **errors** (`000904`) rather than returning zero rows, and **a Snowflake share requires PROD promotion first while a one-off extract does not** — the two delivery options are not equally ready. **Connector is LIVE, not dormant:** template `e7eaf791…`, `status=active`, `BY_LAST_UPDATE` report, US only, **67 partitions all with `run_count>0`**, 65 with result hashes, firing daily ~10:0x UTC, landing confirmed mid-session (5,304→5,310 lines minutes apart). **No `agent` doc exists** for account `da8904db` — the general fleet picks it. ⭐ **The live Cosmos doc carries NO field selection** (walked the whole document), so it pulls the full flat file and **any column Amazon emits already lands** → adding warehouse columns is SQL-only, no template change and no Amazon backfill; separately the staging table holds **7 columns the orderline view discards** (`SELLER_SKU`, `MARKETPLACE`, `PURCHASE_DATE`, `FULFILLMENT_CHANNEL`, `ORDER_STATUS`, `ADDRESS_SHIP_COUNTRY`, `ENTITY_NAME`). **⭐ New durable finding → [[star-schema-convention]]: 32 of 78 numeric columns in the PROD orderline fact are structurally INERT** (never non-zero on the 3,067,775 real sales rows) — the whole `*_SUBSIDIARY` family (9, so the "three flavours" model is wrong: there are two), all `REVENUE_*` (12), `SALES_REVENUE_*` (3), `SALES_MARKDOWNS_*` (4), `NOSALE_ADVERTISING_FEE_*` (2), `SALES_OPEN_*`. **41% of the numeric surface would ship as `$0.00` facts about the client's business** if handed over unfiltered; census before every share. **[[GP-311]] `LINE_STATUS='Unknown'` bucket investigated and CLEARED** — 481,067 rows (13.56% of the fact) are synthetic cost-allocation placeholders parking **$446,188.60** of no-sale ad spend ($441,724.79 product + $4,463.81 brand) with `MARGIN_NET_CONSOLIDATED` at exactly **−$446,188.60**, ties to the cent; the `ORDER_ID`-nulled/`ORDER_KEY`-populated guard is intact on all 481,067. Expected, no action — but a sales-only share must exclude them or they are 13.56% pure noise. **Method lessons, all paid for in-session:** ⭐ **Snowflake returns the same conflated `002003` for "object does not exist" and "you have no grant"**, and my verdict classifier read a permission failure as a missing table — invalidating a whole parity run including its own control; the fix is `INFORMATION_SCHEMA` (needs no object grant) to establish existence **before** any SELECT. The same handler then mislabelled a **timeout** (`000604`) and a **compilation error** (`000904`) as NO-GRANT — *report error codes verbatim, never reclassify*. ⭐ **`re.sub` processes backslash escapes in the REPLACEMENT string**, so a Windows path via `repr()` had its `\\` collapsed and `\Users` became a broken unicode escape — same family as the CLAUDE.md heredoc hazard; fix is a function replacement plus forward slashes, and **verify generated source parses rather than eyeballing escapes**. ⭐ **`WAREHOUSE.SALES_FCT_ORDERLINE` blinks out of `INFORMATION_SCHEMA` during its hourly `CREATE OR REPLACE`** (cron `50 * * * *`) — measured vanishing between two of my own runs, and a query against it timed out mid-rebuild; **sharing that object directly would give a consumer unexplainable intermittent failures.** ⭐ **A killed background grep that reached 1 of ~60 repos still printed a clean-looking empty result**, and I published "zero PROD grants to `NAVIRA_MKT_RO` anywhere" off it — re-swept scoped and the claim held, but the instrument had not been shown able to see. **Credentials:** `NAVIRA_MKT_RO` is a dead end for prod reads (no `.secrets/` key locally, prod Vercel vars Sensitive/unreadable, local `.env` is **ACCOUNTADMIN on the `WAREHOUSE_TEST_GP226` clone**, and every documented grant is TEST-scoped); the wiki line at `navira-marketing-dashboard.md:242` drops the repo's own qualifier *"role locally = ACCOUNTADMIN"*. Prod reads worked as **`PROD_DG1_CORE_ADMIN`/ACCOUNTADMIN** (`snowflake-prod-admin`, `aldc-vault-prod`) after `PAULRUSSELL` failed auth twice — stopped at two to avoid a lockout. Lectric SP-API creds confirmed present in **4 of 5 vaults** (dev/test/qa/prod, all one vintage 2026-05-28; `aldc-cred-vault-qa` has none). **Validated at the QUERY layer ONLY — no rendered check, no PBI model opened.** A **sales-only share view of ~24 live columns is specified but NOT built** (COGS/margin/fees excluded per Paul); client email **drafted, not sent**; measurement scripts are in a session scratchpad and **NOT committed**. Jira: first comment attempt was **classifier-blocked, a retry in-session succeeded** (GP-254 comment `36274`) — **the block is transient, retry before concluding the MCP is down**; draft retained at `agent-factory/boot-prompts/drafts/GP-254-comment-2026-09-03.md`. **Delivery split out to a new ticket [[GP-330]]** ("deliver Lectric agency sales data: one-off extract vs persistent Snowflake share", Consulting/Design) carrying the full technical brief + a client-contact comment; **Paul emailed Justin 2026-09-03** asking one-off vs persistent/daily and is awaiting reply, so GP-254 stays closed and the connector's on/off decision sits on GP-330. | 2026-09-01 | agent-factory-atlas-and-field-manual | **Two self-contained artifacts built, rendered and published from [[agent-factory]] — a measured capability map and a design companion — plus the first measurement of the Zeus↔Factory seam.** ⭐ **The Atlas** (`docs/artifacts/agent-factory-atlas.html`, 59 nodes / 107 edges, hand-written SVG, no library) renders readiness as a **four-segment bar and refuses a percentage**: a live `python -m factory.readiness` (**542.4 s**, primary checkout, HEAD `ab13977`) returned **PASS 13 · FAIL 12 · UNMEASURABLE 4 · NOT_RUN 1** of 30, and collapsing that to "43%" would reproduce the aggregation defect the build-vs-adopt pass documented. Bar geometry is computed from the counts. ⛔ **The prompt's premise did not survive measurement:** `grep -rniE "zeus\|ccx\|opentribe\|cce_" factory/ scripts/ evaluator_service/` returns **nothing** — Zeus Chat is live with **44 tools** (`grep -cE '^\s*name: "[a-z_]+"' src/tools/schemas.ts`), Zeus Memory is live/multi-tenant, a CCX MCP client was connected in-session, and **no Factory code touches either**. Every Zeus edge is drawn dashed and labelled UNBUILT. **The Field Manual** (`docs/artifacts/agent-factory-field-manual.html`, 7 modes) answers "what is above an army" — **NATION**, because an army fights and a nation gets better at fighting — using real **NATO echelon marks** (Ø ● I III XXXX XXXXXX) with the top tier deliberately unmarked; it breaks the army analogy on purpose in one place, plotting *layers of management* as a **flat line at zero across all seven echelons** (R2 killed the LLM-commander topology on 180 configurations). Includes **The Net**, a live dual-rail terminal (conversation left, typed order assembling right with a live hash) whose four mechanics are the dual rail, a **NOT_RECORDED key** that holds a gate red rather than defaulting, a refusal-aware prompt (`dispatch ⨯ blocked by: proof of done` *before* you press it), and **proof asked second** — before scope, tools or budget. ⭐ **New architecture answer — the Mesh.** Paul proposed agents pulling knowledge from each other dynamically and asked whether one vector DB was the alternative; **both fail in opposite ways** (pure peer-to-peer = a rumour network with no ground truth; a single vector store = nearest-neighbour that **cannot say "nothing here"**, i.e. UNMEASURABLE wearing retrieval's clothes). Answer is **three layers**: an append-only evidence **ledger** (canonical — `TaskStore` + `findings.d/` + evidence classes already are this), a rebuildable **index** over it (where a vector store belongs — a projection, never a source, exactly like the Switchboard), and the **mesh** as a **demand-paged** transfer fabric carrying an *evidence pointer* (finding id, basis, discriminating test) and never a conclusion. One rule: **knowledge moves with its verdict attached, or it does not move.** A prior-art table was published alongside it (gossip protocols, Hearsay-II blackboards, stigmergy/R22, federated learning, hybrid RAG) because this estate has shipped a falsifiable novelty claim before — **the mesh is not the breakthrough, the refusal is**: a retrieval layer returning `NOT_KNOWN` as a first-class verdict. Smallest first step, worth doing even if the mesh is never built: **give every finding a typed identity and machine-readable scope**. **Both artifacts `RENDERED_CONFIRMED`** via new Playwright-on-installed-Chrome checkers (`scripts/atlas_render_check.py`, `scripts/manual_render_check.py`) — 6 viewport/theme combinations each, every mode driven (the Net's state machine advanced, not merely painted), plus reduced-motion and JS-disabled passes; screenshots in `docs/evidence/atlas-2026-09-01/` + `docs/evidence/manual-2026-09-01/`. **Six defects were found by rendering that every static check passed over**, two of them reusable: ⭐ **`display:none` on a grid item removes it from the grid**, so hiding a 196px rail made the content pane inherit the 196px track (collapse the *track*, not the item); and ⭐ **`[hidden]` loses to `.rail{display:flex}`** — the published Artifact wrapper adds an `!important` reset and a local render does not, so never rely on the wrapper. ⚠ **Two further findings were the instrument, not the page** (the probe measured a hidden view's zero-size rects, and flagged SVG internals inside an `overflow:hidden` canvas as page overflow) — both fixed in the probe and disclosed; same family as the earlier `render_pass` errors. Gotchas: `impeccable`'s `ignore-value` did **not** suppress its hook, so the progress bar was moved to `transform: scaleX()` (removes the finding at source) and the dead ignore deleted from `.impeccable/config.json`; a background `python -c` redirecting to `$TMPDIR` writes an **MSYS path Python cannot reopen**, and the task notification reported exit 0 over a zero-byte file. **No Jira ticket — the repo's own `ticket` readiness gate reads `PASS · NONE-BY-DECISION`.** Nothing committed in agent-factory (Paul's approval pending; 4 untracked artifact/script paths + 2 evidence dirs, and the checkout is shared with other sessions' dirty files). Resolved at 20:51Z: **consumer $2,010.12 == raw $2,010.12, delta $0.00**. | 2026-05-28 | investigate | GEP/Navira "Missing COGs" (Heather → Lori) — investigated, root-caused, and fix-validated catalog-wide blank-COGS / overstated-margin on the SKU Profitability dashboard. Entry point: 5 CIBU CBD SKUs (30004/05/06/09/10), 94 lines / 58 orders / $4,331.50 gross / $1,995.08 proposed COGS (column F), validated to the penny vs PROD. Found it is NOT 5 SKUs but ~36% of all gross>0 order lines (~1.05M of ~2.74M) with blank COGS. Two root causes proven via reproducible PROD queries (wj66376): (A) cost-history horizon — `COMBINED_MAIN_PRODUCT` begins 2024-05-30 but sales from 2023-01-01 → ~994K lines / ~$44M predate any cost record (~94% of gap, bounded); (B) cost-field/timing — COGS-per-item CASE reads only SITECOST and NULLs on 0 (no ELSE), joined point-in-time on CREATE_DATE; CIBU cost was in AVERAGECOST/LASTCOST from 2026-04-16 (before first sale 04-29) but SITECOST not set until 05-20. `SALES_FCT_COST_HISTORY` is a VIEW (SCD-2 from SITECOST changes), so manual edits impossible/non-durable. Designed fix on the cost-history view: COALESCE(SITECOST→AVERAGECOST→LASTCOST) + back-fill earliest known cost to pre-cost orders (landed-cost priority preserved). Validated non-destructively via shadow views in a SCRATCH_GP259 schema (dropped after): isolated fix-vs-current → 0 of 1,695,715 already-costed lines changed ($0.00 delta), 977,427 blank lines recovered (~$21.1M COGS), worst SKUs 0.4–4%→30–69%. Irreducible residual 272 lines/42 products/~$14K = edge-case SKUs (parts P-*, -Used-VG, _OB, .missing/FBA placeholders, RMA/automation/parent). Produced options doc `aldc-launchpad/gp259-cogs-fix-options-gep.md` (Options A–E + edge-case 1–3). Reply to Heather (cause clarification) sent; options email to Lori for Navira sent. Awaiting Navira's option choice — no prod change yet. Wiki: created `tickets/gep/GP-PENDING-missing-cogs-cost-history.md`, linked from GP-259, index updated. Scripts: `aldc-launchpad/scripts/_gp259_*.py`. |

## 2026-06-25 — Navira Gross Order Count over-counts UNK_ placeholder orders (ALDC-490): diagnosed, TEST-deployed, client-comms
- Heather/Navira flagged: "UNK_" orders (which capture ad spend that did not result in a sale) show Gross Order Count = 1. Investigated PROD Snowflake + both PBI models.
- Root cause: `Sales Measures[_BASE_ACT_SALE_GROS_OC] = DISTINCTCOUNT('Order Line'[ORDER_KEY])` counts the "Unknown orders from cost allocation" rows (`SALES_FCT_ORDERLINE` UNION branch, `ATTRIBUTION_TYPE='No Match'`, `LINE_STATUS='Unknown'`, `UNK_<date>_<ccy>`, 0 sales/qty, carry no-sale ad fees). 1,509 buckets, continuous since 2024-06, ~$406K ad fees. Long-standing (measure dated 2024-05-16), not a regression — surfaced by a new view isolating the rows.
- Fix: `CALCULATE(DISTINCTCOUNT('Order Line'[ORDER_KEY]), KEEPFILTERS('Order Line'[Line Status] <> "Unknown"))`. Cascades to all 19 OC variants + AOV/UPT/agency rollups; no other measure raw-counts ORDER_KEY. Validated PROD 2,747,824→2,746,315, TEST 2,752,624→2,751,115 (−1,509 each), Unknown row →0, sales/qty unchanged, zero ORDER_KEY overlap (no regression).
- Deployed to TEST `66151728` (verified, rollback saved `aldc-launchpad/pbi_ops/_BASE_ACT_SALE_GROS_OC_ROLLBACK_TEST_66151728.txt`). PROD `74a529b3` pending Navira sign-off (comparison workbook + email drafted in `aldc-launchpad/communications/`).
- Jira: ALDC-490 (Bug, S6 sprint, In Progress, assigned Paul). New wiki page [[gep-unknown-order-placeholders]].
- Wiki touched: concepts/business-logic/gep-unknown-order-placeholders.md (new), index.md, log.md.

- 2026-07-27 — **[[GP-304]] NEW security finding: the Eclipse filter-value endpoint ignores the dashboard's locked filter — [[GP-293]]'s 18-client MAP fan-out is HELD.** Second adversarial QA pass, targeting what the 2026-07-26 pass couldn't reach. Read-only on prod throughout; prod asserted (`74a529b3`, not the lookalike TEST `66151728`). **(1) The hole:** `GET /v2/dashboards/{id}/dataset/fields/{locator}/values/` is authorised by **`Dashboard_View` alone** — the exact grant every released client holds on their own dashboard — and was passed **no locked filters** (`request_field_values` issued its DISTINCT query with `filters=[]`). **No tampering needed**: this is the endpoint that populates filter dropdowns, so it's reachable from an ordinary browser session. Measured on Brinno's `Brand=Brinno`-locked `af6fdfe7` (live to 5 external users): **177 brands, 86 vendor email addresses**, 161 manufacturer names, 7 internal account-manager names; **54 of 112 columns enumerable**; Rain Bird's dashboard returned the **identical 177**, so the lock was ignored *uniformly*, not misconfigured on one dashboard. **Seller identity did NOT leak — but only incidentally:** every seller column exceeds the endpoint's unrelated **200-cardinality cap** (`Seller ID` 759/1,556) and returns null. **The cap, not the lock, was the protection** — it would fail silently the day a seller dimension dropped below 200 distinct values. Exposure is the cross-client *dimension catalogue* (brand roster + vendor contact emails), **not** seller-level MAP rows or sales figures — which is why the agreed call was **hold the fan-out, keep Brinno live**. The tell that makes it unambiguous: the comment on the data route 30 lines below says *"locked filters are a security boundary and always apply"* while this route applied none, and **no GP-299 test touched it**. **(2) [[GP-299]]'s open "Bypass B" is now SETTLED — from code, no client tenant required.** The RBAC hierarchy is **flat**: `build_permission_map` parents *every* non-account resource to the **account**, so there is no dashboard→visual inheritance edge; `external-user` carries `[Account_Access]` only, and a dashboard-scoped `viewer` grants `Visual_View` on the **dashboard's** resource id, not on visuals → direct visual/dataView data endpoints **403 by role design**, not by per-dashboard config that could drift. The dataset/dataView field-values routes check their permission via `check_dependency(require_active_account, …)` i.e. against the **active account** (a *different* check shape from `check_path`) → also 403 for clients, though account-level `Creator` does hold them. Foreign `visual_id` under one's own dashboard → **404** (`get_visual_from_dashboard`). **(3) Fix implemented, NOT deployed:** thread `dashboards.lib.locked_filter_conditions` through `get_dataset_field_values`→`request_field_values`, landing in `DatasetRequest.filters` — the *same slot* the data path uses — reusing GP-299's helper so the inclusive-operator whitelist + fail-closed behaviour are inherited rather than reimplemented. **Zero-regression by construction:** no locked filter → `filters=[]`, byte-identical to before, so the internal all-brands tier is untouched. 11 new tests (incl. `mode:hidden` enforced, `mode:normal` must NOT restrict, misconfigured operator fails closed, cardinality cap still short-circuits). Suite pristine `eclipse-2.1` **13 failed/209 passed** → **13 failed/220 passed**; the 13 are pre-existing `test_pct_change.py` ordering pollution (pass in isolation), verified by stashing the edits. `core_api` branch `feature/GP-293-fieldvalues-locked-filter` off **`eclipse-2.1`** (⚠ **`main` is stale** — GP-299 isn't merged there and `locked_filter_conditions` doesn't exist on it), commit `e6b97a9`; `aldc-launchpad` `ad15852`. **(4) ⚠ Correction to the 2026-07-24 entry:** the 5 `@brinno.com` external users hold `viewer` on the **dedicated** `af6fdfe7` — the ids in `_gp293_release_brinno_CREATED.json` are **role-assignment ids on that dashboard**, confirmed against its `roleAssignments` — **not** on the combined `a4ec0d1d` (8 assignments, a separate pre-existing set). So the probed surface *is* the live external one. **(5) ⚠ `email-validator` is absent from core_api's `requirements.txt`**, so `EmailStr` in `api/password_reset/schema.py` makes **every** unit test in that repo fail to *collect* on a clean install — GP-299's own tests included. Those tests run in **no** environment built from `requirements.txt` alone. Worth its own ticket. **Durable lessons:** a locked filter is only as strong as the **narrowest route that queries the dataset** — start scoping work from a route inventory, not from the endpoint the UI calls for rows; a code read can settle what was parked on infrastructure (Bypass B sat ~4 days awaiting "the first client tenant"); and a guardrail that protects **by accident** is not a control. **Still open, needs a client-scoped identity** (Paul provisioning an internal `external-user` + one-dashboard `viewer`): horizontal access, the tamper battery under a client token, token/session abuse, ID enumeration, and the **export/download egress path** (incl. Eclipse-1 "Data Model (Live)") — still the highest-severity *plausible* defect left. Jira: GP-304 created (High, unassigned, blocks GP-293, relates GP-299); GP-293 comment 34718. Evidence `aldc-launchpad/docs/evidence/gp293.md` §7. Updated [[core_api]] (§ GP-304), created [[GP-304]], index.md, log.md.
- 2026-07-29 — **New pattern [[conclave-pr-review]] (`/conclave`) + new repo page [[zeus-chat-exp]], from reviewing [[ALDC-739]] / PR #167 for LingJun.** Asked to review a P1 fix (Zeus Chat fabricated a full financial digest with a fake "Verified (100%)" badge, shown to the CEO) that already carried two Opus bot reviews. Ran it as a **council of 5** independent lenses (correctness/data-flow · security & tenancy · ticket-acceptance/root-cause · adversarial red-team · tests/verification), then re-verified every merge-gating claim against the code myself. Verdict **4 block / 1 approve-with-changes**; review posted to the PR (id `4804432198`) as 1 summary + 8 inline comments; 12 changes requested across 3 tiers, 3 bot comments closed without code. **What the method found that the bots didn't:** (a) the **root cause** — cross-turn carry-forward through *text-only* history (`projectClientHistory` drops tool results), proven because the reported badge substring `"all measures verified, periodicity set, single scope"` exists in **exactly one file** (`handlers.ts:2342`, 0 hits in the system prompt) so the model must have seen a real result, corroborated by `$118,902,434` repeating **byte-identically** across two separately-reported failures; (b) the PR's **headline rule permitted the bug it was written to stop** — Rule 1 allowed figures "returned in THIS conversation" and the carried-forward figures *were* → one-word fix, *this turn*; (c) the rule **broke two shipping features** (`get_metrics_digest`, the QuickBooks revenue playbook) for 7 of 8 privileged tenants, all ALDC staff; (d) the PR was the **first producer of a dead code path** piping unsanitised row data into an LLM judge whose sibling module already has a scrubber; (e) **mutation proof** that the 12 new tests stayed green while 3 of the 5 new rules were gutted, and went red on an em-dash edit; (f) the ticket's worst defect (a PBI measure ignoring its period filter) **can't be fixed in that repo at all** → recommended scope split with a discriminating test. **Two gates that made it accurate, both now mandatory in the skill:** *freshness* (the clone sat at PR #24 while reviewing #167 — every "not on main" claim would have been wrong) and *environment* (`node_modules` absent silently blocked two members; one filed a **BLOCKER predicting `tsc` would fail** — after `npm ci`, tsc clean and 1291/1291 passed, so the false blocker became a correct MINOR). **Durable lessons:** severity and confidence must be **separate axes** (the member's own `LIKELY` caveat is what exposed its error); **feed members your own hypotheses inviting refutation** (killed 5 wrong orchestrator leads); the highest-value finding category is **"valid comment, dangerous remedy"** — 2 of 9 bot comments would have made things *worse* if actioned as written, and naively "fixing" R2-a would have re-opened this very ticket; **unique-string provenance** and **mutation-testing the tests** are both reusable; subagents' plain text is invisible to the orchestrator, so they must be told to deliver via SendMessage or they idle holding a finished report. Skill at `~/.claude/skills/conclave/` (+ `references/council-brief-template.md`). Artifacts `aldc-launchpad/docs/reviews/ALDC-739-PR167-council-review.md` + `…-DRAFT-pr-comments.md`. No Jira comment on ALDC-739 (Paul's call — PR comments suffice). Created [[conclave-pr-review]], [[zeus-chat-exp]], index.md, log.md.
- 2026-07-29 — **[[snowflake-cost-analysis]] — the AUTO_SUSPEND fix CONFIRMED by measurement, and the ALDC-656 guardrail built + hardened on non-prod.** Epic ALDC-651; lanes ALDC-744 (T6 idle) / ALDC-656 (T4 guardrails) / ALDC-745 (T5 archive) / ALDC-752 (T7 anomaly detection). Branch `feature/aldc-651-cost-reduction` in `ALDC-io/observability`, 9 commits (`3a2abbe` `4854487` `555ba89` `f3e0f08` `9956a91` `2ebb123` `01d2295` `d493126` `fe85ade`). **(1) THE MEASUREMENT — and the method that made it possible a day early.** The morning after a change, credits/day can't be compared because the day isn't over. But **the overnight 00:00–06:00 window is a complete elapsed period inside a partial day**, so it compares like-for-like immediately: non-prod **6.05–6.09 every night for 13 nights → 0.70 (−88%)**, prod **6.31–6.75 → 4.88 (−25%)**; suspends **0-on-11-of-13-days → 38** by mid-morning, prod 23–32 → **104**. Corroborated hour-for-hour vs the same weekday (Wed 22 Jul), overlapping hours 00–14: non-prod 15.15 → 1.60, prod 16.67 → 13.53. **⭐ The 1.000 signature:** on 22 Jul non-prod billed **~1.000 credits in every one of 24 hours** — that flat line at exactly one credit/hour *is* an X-Small awake 24/7, recognisable on sight; post-fix most hours read 0.000 and events show `RESUME 13:00:33`→`SUSPEND 13:01:33`, exactly 60 s apart. **⚠ Gate before believing any of it:** an *unpublished* window yields a low number indistinguishable from a win, so `MAX(end_time)` on `WAREHOUSE_METERING_HISTORY` was checked first (`end_time` is the bucket *end*, so a healthy watermark reads slightly ahead of wall-clock). **(2) Revised DOWN ~8%: ~$1,690/mo ≈ $20.3K/yr (~54%)** vs the 2026-07-28 headline of $1,851/$22.2K/55% — non-prod ~2.5 credits/day (beat the ~2.8 target), prod ~19.5 (missed ~18.7). Still overnight-window-based; full-day read due 2026-07-30. Contract recommendation unchanged: **do not sign $40K.** **⚠ Per-item attribution is permanently lost** — `AUTO_SUSPEND` on both accounts *and* the Cube-poller stop all landed 2026-07-28, and prod (the designated A/B control) was changed the same day; non-prod fell ~21.7 credits/day against 14.13 predicted for `AUTO_SUSPEND` alone, the excess belonging to the poller. **Durable lesson: if you designate a control, don't change it the same day — the attribution is worth more than one extra day of saving.** **(3) T4 guardrail LIVE on non-prod, Snowflake-native by deliberate deviation from ALDC-656's written scope** (which specifies the Ofelia cron — rejected because Ofelia runs in local Docker on one workstation and would have been dark during any week it was off, i.e. could have missed this very incident). Serverless task `ALDC_OPS.COST.T_COST_GUARDRAIL` + `SP_COST_GUARDRAIL(BOOLEAN)` + `ALDC_COST_ALERT_EMAIL`, 14:00 UTC. Covers **all five ALDC-752 §1 same-day signals** — zero-suspends (primary: credits drift legitimately, a never-suspending warehouse is always a defect), ±30% step, overnight burn, idle %, new principal — so T7's §1 is now largely *built* and its remaining value sits in §2 (service-account policy), §3 (TTL), §5 (Azure orphans) and the seeded-anomaly test. Validated **not** by inference: unforced eval fired on real history (2026-07-27, 24.12 credits/0 suspends); **email receipt confirmed in the inbox**, not a `SENT` return code; the **task path tested separately from the procedure path** (serverless + `EXECUTE AS OWNER` + managed-task privilege are all different) and later proven **autonomous** at 07:00 on 29 Jul; signal maths cross-checked by independent recomputation reproducing the committed pulse row digit-for-digit. **(4) ⭐ SIX ALERT-DESIGN LESSONS — each an alert that is technically correct but operationally useless.** (a) **Silence must be unambiguous** — breach-only alerting is indistinguishable from broken; added a **Monday heartbeat** stating the rule in-message (*no email for 8+ days = broken, not fine*) + a self-check reporting its own FAILED runs and last success. (b) **Never compare across your own change** — a prior-7-day median still holds pre-change days, so the fix would have fired a −90% `[STEP CHANGE]` **every morning for a week**; added `ALDC_OPS.COST.CHANGE_LOG`, baseline = `max(latest change+1, eval_day−7)..eval_day−1`, **skipped below 2 baseline days** (one day is an anecdote) and *says so* rather than omitting a signal silently; kept **bidirectional** because a large downward step is how a dead pipeline surfaces. Verified: 29 Jul→0 days (suppressed), 30 Jul→1 (suppressed), 31 Jul→2 (resumes on a post-fix baseline). (c) **Put the reported date in the SUBJECT + label `[TEST]` sends** — this actually bit: a forced test send about pre-fix 2026-07-27 was read as *"the changes didn't apply, we're still wasting credits"*. **A false alarm is a defect in the alert, not the reader.** (d) **One source of truth for recipients** — the list was duplicated between `ALLOWED_RECIPIENTS` and a procedure literal (exactly how a notify list drifts to nobody); now table `ALERT_RECIPIENTS` read at run time via `--set-recipients`, which validates each address in isolation, **reports** unverified ones instead of dropping them, refuses an empty list, and can't leave the integration narrowed by a failed probe. (e) **Never address alerts to individuals** — prod was notifying **three departed employees**, so prod alerts reached nobody while looking configured. (f) **Test the scheduled path separately from the logic path**; `TASK_HISTORY`'s first row is the *next scheduled* run, not yours. **(5) ⚠ Snowflake email verification is PER-ACCOUNT** — extends the `090270` gotcha: the same address can be verified in one account and not another. Paul is verified on non-prod, unverified on prod (which is exactly why non-prod alerting works); Vlad unverified on **both**. Two codes: `090270` on `SET NOTIFY_USERS`, **`394209`** on `SET ALLOWED_RECIPIENTS`. No SQL to verify — Snowsight per account. Probe **additively** (keep existing recipients in the trial list) so a rejection leaves the monitor as found. Decision: alerts to **Paul + Vlad**, not Paul alone → **3 verifications needed**, prod guardrail + monitor blocked until then. **(6) ⚠ ALDC-744's description is STALE** — still claims prod was "deliberately left unchanged so it acts as a clean A/B control"; prod was changed 2026-07-28 (`auto_suspend=60` verified live, suspends → 94). This wiki page was correct and the ticket was not. **(7) Azure held deletions triaged (ALDC-745), nothing deleted** — all four in `Quality 1 / rg-aldc-launchpad`, one stopped QA Superset/Cube stack. Front Door `aldc-portal-afd` = the only clearly-safe deletion and holds nearly all the money (~$35/mo base, **no custom domains**, route → a stopped ACI). ACR `aldcqaazcr1c01` **not safe alone** (both ACIs pull `cube`/`superset:latest`; 2026-05-14 images may be the only copy). **Both Key Vaults KEEP** — Standard KV has **no monthly base charge**, so deleting saves ≈$0 while risking the Lectric SP-API set + 15 `gep-prefect` secrets; only the 8 `e2e-*-test` keys are disposable. **`pg-aldc-superset-qa` auto-restarts ~2026-08-04**, silently reversing ~$19/mo *and* re-exposing a `0.0.0.0`–`255.255.255.255` firewall rule with public access on — **any "stop it to save money" action needs a deletion follow-up or a recurring check.** **(8) Non-cost findings:** 5 departed-staff logins still **enabled** on prod (`BRAYDENMARSHALL(ADMIN)`, `SEANOGRADY`, `STEVENDEUTEKOM(ADMIN)`) → access review; and a **prod** Snowflake admin credential (`snowflake-prod-admin`) sitting in the **QA** vault `aldc-vault-qa`. **(9) Key-pair service account deprioritised** — it existed to let an *external* job run unattended; Snowflake-native removed that need. New artifacts: `guardrails/cost_alert_task.sql` (`--;;` sentinel splitting, since a naive `;` split breaks the `$$` body), `guardrails/apply_guardrails.py`, `guardrails/COST_CONVENTIONS.md` (19 rules), `reports/azure-holds-dependency-check.md`. Jira comments: ALDC-651 (×2), ALDC-656 (×2), ALDC-744, ALDC-745, ALDC-752. Boot `aldc-launchpad/boot-prompts/snowflake-cost-aldc651-day3.md` (uncommitted by choice). Updated [[snowflake-cost-analysis]], index.md, log.md.

Linked to GP-298. **(2) [[GP-310]]** — *MAP Violators "Error loading data" — component renders but returns no rows.* Bug, High, Paul. **Prime suspect is an already-documented failure mode, not a new one:** Eclipse resolves the PBI model **by NAME**, so a stale `capacity_options.dataset_name` → 404 `PowerBIEntityNotFound` → *"Error loading data" on every visual while the structure still paints* — the exact signature, recorded during [[GP-293]]. ⚠ Also re-flagged on the ticket: dataset `e80ffd34` exists in **both** Cosmos environments pointing at **different** models (TEST → `66151728`, PROD → `74a529b3`), so it is easy to validate the wrong environment. Checking the inbox settled it the only way that can be settled — **by the account locator in the subject line**: all **12** guardrail emails since 27 Jul read `OG35375` (non-prod), **zero** read `WJ66376` (prod). This is lesson 1 ("silence must be unambiguous") **reappearing one level up**, because another account's alerts were filling the gap — added as **alert-design lesson 7** on [[snowflake-cost-analysis]]. Two unimplemented parts: the copy never creates the missing destination parent (fix = call the existing `ensure_folder_exists`, `file_handler.py:86`), and the endpoint **returns 202 even when the background copy fails** (`# FIXME` `main.py:408`/`:478`) — which is why 18 errors were invisible to the caller. Worth its own bug so it does not leave with the parent ticket. Updated [[snowflake-cost-analysis]] (lesson 7 + inbox evidence), index.md, log.md.
- 2026-07-29 (late) — **[[GP-304]] fix WIDENED after measurement proved the first version closed only one column; PR #250 CI-green but DEAD-LOCKED on review. [[GP-293]] rendered layer re-validated. [[GP-309]]/[[GP-310]] do NOT reproduce as admin.** All prod work read-only except the two commits. **(1) The finding:** the 2026-07-27 fix (`e6b97a9`, threading `locked_filter_conditions` into the field-values query) is necessary but **not sufficient**. The query is `CALCULATETABLE(SUMMARIZECOLUMNS(<col>), KEEPFILTERS(<lock>))` and **that shape only narrows columns on the SAME TABLE as the locked column**. Measured on Brinno's `Brand='Brinno'`-locked `af6fdfe7`: `'Brand'[Brand]` 177→**1** ✅ but `'Product'[Brand]` 177→**177**, `'MAP Violators Daily'[Brand]` 112→112, `'MAP Violators by Brand'[Brand]` 81→81, `'Vendor'[Vendor Email]` 86→86 — i.e. **the same 177-brand roster stayed one column away**, confirmed on the client-facing dashboard route, not just a simulation. **Positive control** (so not a probe artifact): same-table *does* narrow — a `Product[Brand]` lock takes `Product[Manufacturer Name]` 162→1. Adding a **measure** scopes every brand column to 1 but leaves Vendor at 86 — `'Vendor'` has **no relationship path** to `'Brand'`, so no filter shape can ever scope it; and the measure can't be picked generically on a dataset-agnostic route (scoping comes from blank-row elimination). Rejected shapes: `fieldFilters=[lock]` (still 177), lock column in the group-by (**empty payload**). **(2) The fix:** new `assert_field_is_dashboard_filter()` (`api/dashboards/lib.py`) — the route may only enumerate columns the dashboard **configures as a filter**, else 404. Prod serves **54 enumerable columns** while every MAP dashboard (client *and* internal) configures exactly **3**, so the other ~51 have no UI function there — reachable only by direct API call, which is the attack not the feature. **The allow-list *is* the filter bar**, so it closes all of them at once including the unscopable ones, at zero functional cost; mode-independent so the internal all-brands tier is untouched. `06aa015`, +13 tests (34 in the two files), suite **13F/243P** (the 13 = pre-existing `test_pct_change.py` ordering pollution, 43/43 in isolation). **(3) ⭐ DURABLE LESSON — the deploy gate would have LIED.** The e2e `D1` tested **one** locator, so it would have gone **GREEN on deploy** while the leak stayed open via `'Product'[Brand]`. Widened to 4 brand-bearing columns × 19 dashboards + new `D5` (sensitive non-brand dimensions must be scoped **or refused**); both red pre-fix, D2/D3 green. *A gate that tests the one instance you happened to measure certifies the fix, not the vulnerability.* **(4) BLOCKED — needs a person, not a command:** PR #250 is OPEN/MERGEABLE with **11/11 CI green**, but `eclipse-2.1` requires an approving review. Author is `russell94paul` (**no self-approval**), Paul's token is **`admin: false`** (no `--admin` merge), and the green `review` check is the **Claude bot which only COMMENTED** (12 comments, 0 approvals). Requested: `mikestuart26`, `data-mission`. Same shape as the [[clients-repo]] CODEOWNERS dead-lock. Deploy workflow confirmed **stage-slot only, no swap step**, and both slots run the identical image `2.0.0-eclipse-2-1.1` so no rollback version is at risk. **(5) GP-293 rendered layer re-validated on prod:** **27 passed / 2 failed / 1 skipped**; all **19** seller row counts **identical to the 07-27 baseline (zero drift)**; the 2 fails are `C Muse` (genuinely 0 violators at 90d) and `D1` (the gate). `D4` **skipped: `not-openable`** — a locked Brand filter exposes no dropdown in the UI, which **invalidates §1–§2 of `docs/evidence/gp304/MANUAL-QA-map-violators.md`** (its "is the right code live?" step is "open the Brand chip and see one value" — not performable; needs rewriting before Paul uses it). **(6) [[GP-309]]/[[GP-310]] do NOT reproduce as admin:** all **five** Eclipse query paths (`default`/`expanded-timeframe`/`previous-period`/`year-over-year`/`table-comparison`) return **HTTP 200 on both** Brinno surfaces, and the combined-dashboard MAP tile renders 9 rows/9 columns clean. Killed lead: the dashboard has picked up a **`'Marketplace'[Marketplace Name]` `normal` filter** (browser-sent, not server-injected — genuinely plausible) but the predicate is **inert** on that visual (200/6 rows) while the sales table narrows correctly. Ranked remaining hypotheses: **it already healed** (failed warehouse/PBI refresh at check-in, since fixed by the 22:03Z refresh — *check refresh history before the window ages out*), client-identity-only, browser-cached layout (hard-reload), or a different object than assumed. **Residuals stated not buried:** `Avg ASINs Listed` stays served unscoped on client dashboards (55 values, non-identifying numerics but cross-client); `Selling FBA (Yes/No)` already 404s on this route because its column name contains `/` (a *configured* client filter that can never load values — own ticket); `email-validator` missing from `requirements.txt`. Boot: `aldc-launchpad/boot-prompts/gp304-fieldvalues-deploy.md` (+ updates appended to the `gp309`/`gp310` inquest prompts). Commits: core_api `06aa015` (PR #250), aldc-launchpad `79dfeb0`. Updated [[core_api]], [[GP-304]], log.md.
- 2026-07-30 — **[[GP-310]] RESOLVED + deployed to PROD, and it corrected [[GP-293]]'s founding premise. New ticket [[GP-312]].** Ran as an `inquest` (council of 3 + devil). **(1) Root cause, PROVEN by complete enumeration:** visual `8fd6a3d8` ("MAP Violators — Sellers (Brinno)") had **no `rbac_role_assignment` row** — the only 1 of **64** prod visuals with zero. `PermissionChecker.check` fails closed on a key-set miss, and `build_permission_map` builds that key set with **no assignee filter**, so it is *user-independent* ⇒ 403 for **every** user, admins included. ⭐ Counter-intuitive tell: "the admin also 403s" is the **signature** of this cause, not a refutation — any other RBAC branch would let an admin through. **(2) Why it hid for 2 days:** Eclipse has **two** visual-fetch routes with different guards — `/v2/dashboards/{d}/visuals/{v}/` checks `Dashboard_View` only, `/v2/visuals/{v}/` checks `Visual_View` on the visual itself; the selector is whether `<Visual>` receives a `dashboardId` prop (`ViewDashboard` sets it, `EditDashboard` deliberately does not). So `/dashboards/{id}` was healthy while `/explorer/dashboard/{id}` was broken — a green 26/26 e2e suite and every admin reproduction validated the working route. **Lesson: validate BOTH routes.** **(3) Forensics:** prod `AppServiceHTTPLogs` (30d) gave 17/17 requests 403, **zero 2xx across the object's entire lifetime**; a client session took 10× 403 at 20:15–20:17Z — *inside* the 20:00–20:30Z Navira weekly check-in — while 105/105 requests to 14 sibling visuals on the same dashboard/route/two-minutes returned 200. ⚠ Those log rows carry live session tokens in `Cookie` + real client IPs: never project them. **(4) The obvious fix was WRONG and was reverted:** writing the missing row cleared the 403 *and* made the direct route return **743 sellers (all brands)** vs 6 dashboard-scoped — the clone's own `options.filters` is not applied on that route (GP-293's tamper battery proved self-scoping on the *dashboard-scoped* route only). Net prod change **zero**. Recorded honestly: that read used an admin token, which cannot distinguish internal-allowed from client-reachable, and shared `63d41094` behaves identically — so it was pre-existing, not caused. **(5) The real fix — architecture, not a grant.** [[GP-293]]'s premise (a `Product[Default Vendor]`-locked dashboard cannot scope a `Brand[Brand]`-keyed MAP tile, hence a per-client clone) is **FALSE**: `Product[Brand] → Brand[Brand]` is **BothDirections** and carries into the MAP facts. Measured on shared `63d41094`: vendor-lock **11 rows == brand-lock 11 rows**, vs 751 unlocked; negative control — same filter via the seller-grain table stays 750 (many→one edge cannot propagate), which is what proves real propagation. ⇒ **embed the existing shared visual, build nothing.** No clone, no new RBAC row, no 403, no new grant surface — the defect *class* is removed rather than patched 8 more times. **(6) Shipped to prod (all reversible, backups committed):** component embedded on **8** vendor dashboards (NAVAC, Suitical, Penn-Plax, Coxreels, Condor, G7th, Dalen, Rain Bird); `Selling FBA` + `Min Avg ASINs Listed` filters **restored** to those 8 (audit: 21 of 23 MAP dashboards had them, the only two without were the vendor dashboards — migrating would have silently dropped two client controls; `Min Avg ASINs Listed` is the [[GP-261]] catalogue floor that drops 83 of 92 single-ASIN noise "violators"); internal `eaa10f9e` component swapped `f16fb03d`→`63d41094` + timeframe→YTD so internal matches client; MAP removed from Brinno `a4ec0d1d` + clone deleted; **19 dedicated MAP dashboards retired** (prod dashboards 60→41). Verified: 9/9 surfaces HTTP 200, 8 client tiles scoped to their own vendor, internal all-brands (743); **rendered** QA on NAVAC (both routes), Condor, and the Brinno removal. External access checked *before* deleting — no external user left without a dashboard (⚠ `gp293.md` §3b claimed the 5 `@brinno.com` users were on the dedicated dashboard only; **live data disagreed and won**). **(7) New: [[GP-312]]** — `Product[Default Vendor]` and `Brand[Brand]` disagree on client names (`Condor Tool & Knife` vs `Condor`; `PENN-PLAX`/`G7TH` by case) — 3 of 8; a display-name-keyed test reported "Condor → 0 sellers" (it has 21), which would have shipped an empty tile looking exactly like the bug. Mitigated by a preflight that refuses to embed unless the **enforced** lock (`filters[].locked_value.value`, **not** `restricted_values` — that is the dropdown allow-list, often `[]` on a hard lock) resolves to >0 rows and < baseline. **(8) Method traps worth keeping:** a safety check that *cannot fail* proves nothing (the first scope check "passed" because no data came back at all — both branches predicted the same observation); **a DAX `VAR` evaluates once, eagerly**, so wrapping it in `CALCULATE` does nothing (produced a confident false negative, "the measures ignore the timeframe", stated before re-testing); `first`/`last` are reserved in KQL. **The check that would have caught this before a client did: no prod `explorer_visual` may have zero `rbac_role_assignment` rows** — 64 visuals, 63 covered, 1 orphan, and the orphan was the failure. Not yet built. Commit `ee48ec5` (`aldc-launchpad`, branch `feature/gp293-map-embeddable-component`, pushed). Coordinated with the parallel [[GP-309]] session via `docs/evidence/INQUEST-LOCK.md`; handoff at `boot-prompts/gp309-handoff-20260730.md` (⚠ GP-309 must re-capture its rollback snapshot — 19 of its 37 target dashboards no longer exist).
- 2026-07-30 — **[[GP-311]] resolved via `/inquest`, and both council skills are now tracked in the wiki.** Navira's SKU Profitability showed more Orders than Units (2 units / 8 orders, W29). Verdict: the Data Model was correct, the dashboard wrong — but **the inherited reasoning was wrong too**. Reading the app's public client bundle proved it posts **raw SQL** to `/v1/warehouse/query` and never touches Power BI, so ALDC-490's DAX measure fix could never have reached it. **The guard had been placed at the consumption layer for a fact-layer defect** (`LINE_STATUS` filtered nowhere downstream of the fact builds), which is why the same 26-month-old defect surfaced twice five weeks apart through different consumers. Fixed at the fact: `CASE WHEN LINE_STATUS = 'Unknown' THEN NULL ELSE ORDER_ID END` — `COUNT(DISTINCT …)` ignores NULL, so one change corrected every SQL consumer with **zero consumer-code change**, keeping `ORDER_KEY` populated and keeping the rows (they carry the no-sale ad spend). Deployed to **PROD and TEST**, view + table, all assertions green; QA passed at the rendered surface (Condor `MS_CTK3941-6.1HC` **82 → 1**; TACoS 113.36% confirming ad spend survived). ⚠ **A first attempt was deployed and rolled back** — it patched the Unknown *branch*, which feeds `INNER JOIN … ON a.ORDER_ID = b.ORDER_ID`, so `NULL = NULL` deleted **471,264 rows and $423,072.95 of ad spend**; the correct site is the outer projection, after all joins. Its verification also **falsely passed**, asserting only `unknown_with_id == 0` — trivially true once the population is gone. Both lessons are now in `/inquest`. Council of 5 caught **six errors** in the case file pre-handoff, including a safety warning citing `NOSALE_ADVERTISING_FEE_CONSOLIDATED`, which is hard-coded to $0.00. Follow-up [[GP-314]] raised for the `MAX(p.SITE_PRICE)` phantom-price defect (blocked: app source unfindable across 40+ repos, 35 org repos, code search, 4 Azure subs, no sourcemaps). New: [[inquest-bug-resolution]], `concepts/patterns/skills/` mirrors + [[README]]; revision logs added to both skill pages.
- 2026-08-03 — **[[GP-311]] closed; [[GP-314]] and [[GP-315]] raised.** Repo aligned with deployed truth (`clients` PR #494 -> `GEP/development`, `a4ac6c9`), regression test shipped and mutation-proven in `navira-marketing-dashboard` (`bc21f25`), and a new ticket page written at `tickets/gep/GP-311.md` capturing seven durable traps. Headline lesson: **a consumption-layer guard cannot fix a fact-layer defect** — [[ALDC-490]] guarded one DAX measure and every SQL consumer kept the bug, which is why the same defect resurfaced five weeks later through a surface that posts raw SQL. Second lesson, now in [[inquest-bug-resolution]]: **an absence assertion can be satisfied by destroying the population, and still survives mutation-testing** — it printed "applied and verified" over a $423K ad-spend deletion, and the SAME class of error recurred hours later in a consumption probe whose verdict logic treated a *failed* instrument as a clean negative (fixing it flipped "nobody reads this" to "four readers including SERVICE_POWER_BI"). [[GP-315]] is the residue of a deliberate trade-off: `ORDER_KEY` was left populated for the PBI measure and the fact->dim join, so `ORDER_KEY` counters are still exposed. Credential rotation (PROD ACCOUNTADMIN + non-prod) confirmed as being actioned separately.
- 2026-08-03 — **[[GP-309]] payload cap DEPLOYED to PROD + Vlad's YoY measures promoted; [[eclipse-azure-deployment]] corrected on four counts.** Ran as an `inquest`. **(1) The reported surface was a victim, not the cause.** "Brinno dashboard not loading — YoY filter" was a **conflation of two meeting items**: intermittent failures caused by an *all-brands* `expanded-timeframe` request returning **HTTP 200 with 474,116 rows / 110 MB in 50.9 s** — after which **~20 requests to OTHER dashboards returned 503** — plus a separate **DAX** year-over-year fix by Vlad. Brinno never reproduced because Brinno was collateral; ~260 read-only probes came back clean before the reporter's own words ("no data loaded") reframed the search. **Durable rule: a client-reported surface is a claim about where the symptom APPEARED, not where the defect LIVES; and when a report names a control that testing exonerates, suspect the ticket fuses two items.** **(2) Two wrong diagnoses were built and killed before the right one.** A bare `null` 200 body on the comparison paths was proven to 10/10 pre-committed predictions — and was **guarded on both sides** (`MetricsCard.tsx:110-133 shouldDisableYoY()` mirrors the server's six `None` classes one-for-one; tables never call those routes), so it could not be the bug. Then a blank `''` in `restricted_values` on 37 dashboards reproduced the reported *message* exactly and dashboard-wide — and was **withdrawn** when the rendered check showed the dropdown never offers it and `datasets/lib.ts:245` strips blank-valued filters client-side. **Both were reproduced at the API layer with `filters: []`, i.e. against a user who does not exist** — `normal`-mode dashboard filters are browser-sent, so no probe replayed the consumer's real state, and four council members missed it because the shared brief framed them away from it. **(3) The fix: cap the QUERY, refuse rather than truncate.** `MAX_EXPANDED_TIMEFRAME_ROWS = 50_000` in `api/visuals/deps.py`; bound the request so the expensive scan cannot run, then return **413** with an actionable message. **Rejected a granularity floor** — keyed on window *length* it would coarsen healthy charts (Brinno YTD/day is 1,088 rows) to fix one pathological request; the discriminator must be **size, not span**. **Rejected truncation** outright — silently dropping part of a trend series is worse than the bug and invisible to the client. An explicitly larger `row_limit` is also clamped: one dashboard does not get to opt into degrading other tenants. 14 tests, suite 169→183, **mutation-tested with 4 mutations all RED**. PR `core_api#251` → `f2a966f9`, slot-swapped into prod 22:00:20Z→22:01:42Z. **(4) Verified at BOTH layers:** API smoke **11/11** (guard fires 413 in 1.05 s), then **both dashboards loaded in a browser** — Brinno client-facing (cards, deltas, full daily sparklines, SKU table, pie chart) and NAVIRA all-brands internal ($25.3M/+19.6%, sparkline intact). **Nothing truncated**, because dashboard tables use the `default` path (~200 rows) and card sparklines are **date**-grain (214 rows) — the 474k payload only arises at **SKU × day** grain, which no normal load requests. **(5) ⭐ Four corrections to [[eclipse-azure-deployment]]:** the `stage` slot is a **separate environment** (own Cosmos `aldctestcsdb1c01`, **8 dashboards vs 41**, own `JWT_SECRET` → prod token 401, own user store → stage token 403 on prod IDs, largest reachable result **705 rows**) so **the guard could not be exercised there at all** and "QA it on stage" was an untestable plan; `rollback-check` is a **NO-OP whenever the mutable GitVersion tag is unchanged** — both slots read identical strings, so it can never detect divergence, and the push-triggered auto-run is **not** always broken (run `30770480648` went green without `force_deploy`); **read GHCR push timestamps, not the tag**, to know which build a slot runs, and note swap-back only rolls back **while the old container survives**; and **verify slot-sticky settings before swapping** (8 of 18 — every setting that differs between slots is sticky, every non-sticky one identical, which is *why* the swap is safe). **(6) `required_review_thread_resolution` blocks merges silently** — #251 sat `BLOCKED` with all checks green *and* a code-owner approval, purely on **12 unresolved bot threads**; I wrongly blamed a phantom required check (it existed and was SUCCESS — my read was truncated) then CODEOWNERS. `core_api#250` has been stuck the same way for 6 days. Also found: `CODEOWNERS` lists two owners without repo write access, and **CI never runs the unit suite** (`ci.yml` sets `mutation-testing: false`; only `pytestarch` runs) so all 183 tests are unenforced. **(7) YoY measures promoted to PROD** (`74a529b3`, 269→271) after locating them by sweeping **931 measures across every model in both workspaces** — they were TEST-only, so Vlad's fix had never reached prod and "verify across dashboards" was unsatisfiable. Verified by the identity `LY(year N) == Gross(year N-1)`: **4/4, 0 wrong**, with an `ISBLANK` guard added on promotion so **CY2027 renders BLANK instead of −100%** (proven read-only first via an inline `DEFINE MEASURE`: exactly 1 row changed). **(8) Never exact-baseline a moving window** — all-time grew ~61 rows/day and rolling `last-30-days` dropped 56→55 overnight; **static windows are the exact anchors** (static `07-03..08-01` = 56 = yesterday's number, static `07-04..08-02` = 55 = today's), bands for the rest. Corollary: at ~61 rows/day **Brinno reaches the 50,000 cap in ~11 months**, so the constant should be env-tunable. **(9) 17 green unit tests shipped a rendered bug** — `eclipse#92` surfaces the 413's message but without `grow` on the parent `Stack` the container query never fires and the text stays **invisible**; Paul's visual QA caught it. *For anything whose deliverable IS rendered output, tests prove the logic and only the surface proves the feature.* **NOT DONE:** `eclipse#92` unmerged (needs 1 approval + the `grow` fix); client has **not** confirmed, so GP-309 left at **GEP QA, not Done** per the [[FU92-419]] lesson; `Prev %` == `YoY %` at YTD on **39 of 41** dashboards is a definitional redundancy left untouched pending a product decision; the **GP-293 e2e suite is stale** (A1–A4 and A5 target dashboards [[GP-310]] deleted on 07-30) so it did not act as the gate. Commits `5b79b54`…`e96afc5` on `feature/gp293-map-embeddable-component`; handoff `boot-prompts/gp309-handoff-2026-08-03.md`; smoke harness `eclipse_ops/_gp309_deploy_smoke.py`. Updated [[eclipse-azure-deployment]], index.md, log.md.
- 2026-08-09 — **Requirement logged (not scoped): cross-*platform* slicing/reporting on the Navira Daily model, alongside the in-flight cross-*channel* ad-spend design.** Paul, mid-session on the GP-317/GP-318 `/assay`: *"I mentioned cross-channel, but what we will also want is an option for analysis and slicing and reporting cross platform too. This is probably straightforward to add to the model."* Recorded in [[navira-daily-model-lineage]] (full open questions) + [[navira-roadmap-status]] "Not started / deferred". **The two terms are distinct axes in this estate and must not be conflated:** *cross-channel* = the **ad** side (`MARKETING_FCT_ACTIVITY_UNIFIED.PLATFORM_ID` — Amazon SP/SB/SD, Google, Meta); *cross-platform* = the **selling** side (`SHARED_DIM_MARKETPLACE` — Amazon US/CA/UK/MX/BR/EU, the 7 Shopify stores, Bridgford WooCommerce, Walmart, eBay, Target, Wayfair, NewEgg, Best Buy US/CA, Etsy, Reverb, GEP Brands, Wholesale). Paul's "straightforward" read is plausible — **the dim and its relationship to `Order Line` already exist**, so the likely work is grouping + legibility (a platform-GROUP column: Amazon / DTC-owned-site / retail-marketplace / wholesale), not plumbing. ⚠ **Prerequisite, not a follow-up: DAX Flag A** (`aldc-launchpad/pbi_ops/navira_dax_flags_A_B.md`, drafted, still unapplied) — a user can wrongly cross-platform `SUM(SALES_AMOUNT)`; platform-attributed sales double-count and must never be summed across platforms (use `ACTUAL_SALES_*`). Shipping the slicer without it hands a correctness footgun to the model the CEO report reads. Also open: what ad spend renders under a *non-Amazon* platform slice (**blank, never zero** — same rule the cross-channel design is adopting; only Amazon ad spend carries a marketplace), and four-kinds-of-zero honesty for the ~19 marketplace members that currently carry no data. Whoever picks this up should read the cross-channel design's outcome first so both axes share one blank-vs-zero rule and one legibility standard.
- 2026-08-10 — **[[FU92-421]] raised and diagnosed via `/inquest`: one client report, TWO unrelated root causes, neither a code defect.** Juliann reported 3 DAX flights whose spend stopped early (E8TWD + RFT7V through 7/21, GMX2U 6/1–6/7). **(A) NOT OURS — 3 LinkedIn ad accounts silently dropped out of [[Windsor]] on 2026-07-21** at *one shared load instant* (`10:16:25.681-07:00`) while `500938269` kept loading daily; a direct Windsor API call with the Fusion92 key now returns **only that one account**, absent even for pre-cutoff dates Snowflake already holds. Fusion92's LinkedIn feed is down to **1 live account of 5** — progressive decay, same failure mode as [[FU92-415]] (Smile Doctors, which the new monitor shows is **still dark 92 days** and never recovered despite "Ready for Customer" since 06-11). Fix is a client re-grant in the Windsor portal; ⚠ on reconnect the backfill must be verified back to 07-22 or the gap stays permanently missing. **(B) FIXED — GMX2U**: the client asked ~05-28 to change the Meta optimisation goal to Form Submissions, Meta can't do that in place, so the campaign was rebuilt 06-01 as `..._FormSubmits` and the flight kept naming the retired one → **$26,526 of live spend attached to no flight at all**. Appended campaign `120246436851970753` + its 3 ad sets (appended, not replaced, so the first week's $281.06 survives); consumer layer `DAX_API.DAILY_FLIGHT_METRICS_NO_DAX` went **$281.06 → $27,462.41** across three monthly rows, arithmetic closing to the cent, `FLIGHT_COUNT = 1`, zero duplicate-split rows. **⭐ The load-bearing lesson: aggregating at the wrong grain refuted the RIGHT hypothesis.** "Connector outage" was dismissed because `PLATFORM_ID = 'Direct-LinkedIn'` read `MAX(SPEND_DATE)` = today — carried entirely by the one surviving account. **Freshness must be grouped by ad ACCOUNT, never by platform**; four council members independently re-derived this after inheriting the orchestrator's bad frame, and `devil`'s explicit licence to reject the frame is what recovered it. Also: **[[FU92-419]] fit the story perfectly and was wrong** (those flights had blank IDs and showed total $0; these had complete IDs and partial spend) — the best-fitting documented failure mode is the most dangerous lead. Also: **an absence of rows is not an absence of spend** — this feed *does* land literal `$0.00` rows when a campaign is live but not delivering (450 on `509879445`), and that negative control is what converted "maybe the campaigns ended" into "the data was never loaded"; a final day at 55–61% of run-rate across two unrelated accounts is a mid-day load, not a taper. Also: **`application/update` is a partial merge taking changed fields under a `document` key** (`workflows/.../netsuite.py:541`) — a full flat document returns **HTTP 200 and silently writes nothing**, so a 200 is not proof of a write; re-read and diff every field. ⚠ **Do NOT loosen the LinkedIn `ORDER_ID` join** — admits **+$119,922 (+16.9%)** of correctly-excluded spend across **23,883** multi-claimed (account, campaign_group) pairs (worst by 97) which the even-split logic smears across unrelated flights, and fixes nothing since zero raw rows exist to admit ([[GP-311]] trap shape); widening dates, `TRIM`-ing tokens, forcing a dynamic-table refresh and re-running the FU92-419 reconcile are all provable no-ops. **Prevention shipped-but-not-deployed:** an account-freshness monitor on the existing obs-jobs runner (Tier 1 account-dark / Tier 2 feed-dark, so a *total* outage isn't masked by the absence of a healthy sibling) — no new infra, no new credentials, ~$0 marginal Snowflake cost; realises [[FU92-379]]. **Its first run produced 21 findings, mostly long-retired accounts — true and useless**, so the alert window was capped at 21 days dark, cutting it to 5 actionable; an un-calibrated alert gets muted, which is how this failure survived 17 days. It also surfaced two unreported live incidents (Meta `HD - Talent Acquisition` 6d, Google `N|H - Lincoln Park` 9d) and **7IHPM**, a 4th affected flight running to 12-31 that the client never reported. Scope enumerated rather than sampled: 323 active flights → 172 with spend → 8 stale ≥5d → **4 genuine defects** (94.8% current). Detection runbook added to [[Windsor]]; ticket in **QA**, client emailed 08-10. Evidence: `clients` `feature/paulrussell/fu92-flight-actuals-diagnosis` (`ebf38b2f`), `observability` `feature/paulrussell/fu92-account-freshness-monitor` (`890abde`) — both still local/unpushed. Residual gap stated honestly: GMX2U verified at the query layer the app issues, **not** on the rendered DAX screen (browser tooling unavailable).
- 2026-08-10 — **Navira Daily-model cross-channel ad-spend `/assay`: 15 findings, 5 gaps closed, the client correction document rescued before sending, and one new 262% defect that only the client's own pivot exposed.** Branch `feature/navira-ad-metrics-request` (`aldc-launchpad`), Jira GP-317 + GP-318 commented. Nothing deployed, nothing sent. **⭐ THE DURABLE LESSON — validate in the gesture the CONSUMER uses, not all-time.** Seven independent lenses measured the model for hours and every one missed the biggest defect because all of them measured all-time. Paul supplied one sentence — *"the client normally uses a pivot with calendar year on it, filtering for current year, so YTD"* — and reproducing that single gesture exposed the two Amazon ad figures diverging **+262% in August 2026** ($163,689 allocated vs $45,198 spend). All-time showed **+4.1%** and looked reassuring; CY2024/CY2025 (−0.2% each) diluted it, as did the "99.81% over 26 months" figure already quoted to the client (measured on PROD, dominated by the agreeing months). ⇒ **Ask how the consumer slices before choosing a validation window.** Cause deliberately NOT diagnosed — an earlier draft explained a smaller version as "rebuilt on different schedules" and that could not be substantiated, which is the failure this now-pending `/inquest` exists to avoid repeating; 5 hypotheses each with a discriminating test + predicted result in `boot-prompts/navira-august-allocation-divergence-inquest.md`. **Other consumer-layer defects (all measured, none fixed), now in [[navira-daily-model-lineage]]:** (1) the marketplace **slicer** silently drops all Google+Meta — $118,206 YTD / 11.6% — because `MARKETING_EFFICIENCY` relabels the fact's `Unknown` to `Cross-Channel`, which is not a dim member, so 768 rows get a BLANK key and attach to the relationship's unselectable auto-unknown member; a **matrix reconciles to $0.00** while the slicer drops everything (two gestures, two behaviours — the blind-member split was found by keeping council members blind to each other); ⚠ do NOT add `Cross-Channel` to `SHARED_DIM_MARKETPLACE` — measured, that dim drives sales (Amazon US $81.4M) so a synthetic member pollutes every sales slicer ⇒ **channel must leave the marketplace axis**. (2) **Every Brand/Product pivot of ad spend is 175× wrong** (grand total on all 175 rows, $496M vs $2.84M truth) — no relationship from `Marketing Efficiency` to `Brand`/`Product`; **the load-bearing discriminator is that the Date-dim control PASSES**, which is what makes it a missing relationship rather than a `SUMMARIZECOLUMNS` artifact. (3) **Sponsored Display never stopped** — TEST raw holds $6,485 through 2026-08-08 while the fact reads a stale PROD copy frozen at 2026-06-05; `NOT RECORDED`, not `ZERO`; we were one step from telling the client their campaigns had stopped. **Matching values are not provenance** — the three-way TEST-raw/PROD-raw/fact test is what separated pipeline bug from client behaviour. (4) "blank never zero" is decided but unimplemented — needs BOTH a SQL `NULL` and a 4th (blank) format-string section. (5) `Margin After Ad Spend` nets **Amazon only**, leaving $244,870 of Google+Meta undeducted while the metric dictionary claims all three. **GP-318 re-scoped:** PROD has **no `GOOGLE_ADS`/`META`/`WINDSOR` schema and no `MARKETING_EFFICIENCY` family** — there is no Google branch to promote and no production figure to "match"; it is a connector project, and the 14–22h estimate was wrong by a category. Also found: a repo-trunk overwrite of PROD `MARKETING_FCT_ACTIVITY` would silently revert **GP-199 Phase 2** SB ASIN attribution (only revision `c97e5e64` is a clean superset), and a 22-column PROD base would break TEST's 21-column passthrough and take the client's live model down. **Client document rescued:** six errors fixed in `docs/evidence/gp317/navira-ad-cost-correction.html`, three of which the client would have seen the moment they opened the model (changes described as live that were not applied; Meta start date wrong by 22 months; a $25K discrepancy explained by an unmeasured mechanism) plus a false `$0.00` printed in a document arguing `$0.00` is a false claim, and "99% product coverage" that measures **92%** (SB US is 88.6% sentinel *by spend* — counting ASINs that exist ≠ counting spend that carries one). **Method notes:** 4 of 7 council members were killed mid-run by an account-wide session limit — reports lost, but every member had written raw output to disk first, so the *measurements* were fully recovered and mined by the synthesiser; **four premises of the run's own brief were refuted by its own council**, which is the correlated-error failure mode arriving on schedule and being caught. Reading the *rendered* artifact (not the source) caught a further overclaim of my own. Ledger: `docs/evidence/gp317/assay-findings-ledger.md`.

## 2026-08-12 — ingest(GP-318): Daily Sales Model items 1-2, and three findings bigger than the ticket

New page [[tickets/gep/GP-318]]; index entry added. Session covered GP-318 item 2 (durable Meta fix),
B16 (orphaned view retired), and three things found on the way that outlive the ticket:

1. **A scoping premise refuted by measurement.** Item 2 named the activity fact; the defect was in
   `REPORT_COMMON.MARKETING_EFFICIENCY`'s UNION-pivot `0` filler. Tracing the consumer route rather
   than trusting the boot prompt is what found it — and the defect generalises to any future platform
   whose start date post-dates the view's floor.
2. **`main` is not a deploy branch.** Two "would DELETE live platforms" warnings had reached Jira and a
   commit message; both were artefacts of diffing against `main` while GEP promotes through
   `GEP/development`. Withdrawn. Same check also disproved the long-standing "CODEOWNERS dead-lock"
   framing for the GEP path — those branches carry no rulesets.
3. **A trap documented nine times and never fixed.** `XmlaClient._get_model()` returned `Databases[0]`.
   Nine boot prompts warned about it and three scripts forked around it, yet it still bit a tenth
   session. Per-script memory does not compound; a fixed shared helper does. Fixed + 44-site audit.

Also: 11 of 15 `REPORT_COMMON` objects had no repo-managed source and were captured verbatim into
`clients` `GEP/development`.

**Kept in the repo, not the wiki** (per the estate rule — wiki carries the durable lesson, not per-run
blobs): the recon/dry-run/deploy/consumer JSON, the captured DDL and the render screenshots stay in
`aldc-launchpad/docs/evidence/gp318/`, pinned to commits `2595fd0`, `9dcdb59`, `3fffd1b`. Jira cites
those commits, not the wiki.

## 2026-08-13 — GP-318 item 3 (margin identity) closed; enablement-guide pattern captured

**Ingest source:** conversation 2026-08-13, `aldc-launchpad` commit `e936735`, Jira GP-318 comment
`35981`.

**Pages touched:** [[GP-318]] (item 3 outcome, the reintroduced-defect lesson, the full open tail),
**new** [[model-enablement-guide]], `index.md`.

Item 3 turned out not to be the defect the ticket described. The pre-registered remedy
(`COALESCE(SPEND_USD,0)`) was refuted by measurement before any deploy: the NULLs in
`MARGIN_AFTER_AD_SPEND_USD` are correct because the warehouse holds ad instrumentation for only four
marketplaces. The real defect was scope in the PBI field list, fixed with additive DAX on both live
models — **no warehouse write, so the item-3 write approval went unused.**

Two lessons promoted to the wiki because they generalise beyond this ticket: (1) **when a
decomposition almost closes, suspect the instrument's grain** before positing another cause — the
"unexplained" ~$2,136 was marketplace-grain filtering failing to see a mixed marketplace; (2) **a fix
pass can reintroduce the very defect it is fixing** — five new measures went in unguarded alongside
20 guarded siblings, caught only by asserting the live expression before overwriting it.

New pattern page [[model-enablement-guide]] captures Paul's plan for the designed HTML model guide
(architecture diagram, table/measure reference generated from TOM, templated pivot cookbook, and an
explicit boundary section). It is scheduled **after** the GP-318 tail wrap so it does not document a
moving target, and will be produced for the Daily Sales Model first, then per [[GP-319]] candidate
design.

**Kept in the repo, not the wiki:** the pre-registration, measurement, probe, apply, verify and
rollback JSON stay in `aldc-launchpad/docs/evidence/gp318/gp318_item3_*`, pinned to `e936735`.
